## 1. Agricultural Land Protection
and Development Act s. 2
Disposition: standalone
Confidence: high
Priority: P1
Reason: Section 2 is one incorporation entry point: it allows formation of an agricultural land owners association and issuance of letters of incorporation on a regulated application.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Formation application for agricultural land owners association; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:2","childLabels":["2(1)","2(2)"],"evidenceText":["may be formed for the purposes of this Act and the regulations","Letters of incorporation forming an agricultural land owners association may be issued upon application"]}]; goal: Recall how an agricultural land owners association may be formed and how letters of incorporation may be issued.

## 4. Agricultural Land Protection
and Development Act s. 12
Disposition: split
Confidence: high
Priority: P1
Reason: Section 12 contains distinct construction topics: applying to build a ditch or drain through adjacent property, deposit-based permission and payouts, and the Minister's investigation, construction, and assessment powers.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Application to construct ditch or drain through adjacent property; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:12","childLabels":["12(1)"],"evidenceText":["necessary for the proper drainage of agricultural land","run a ditch or drain through the property of an adjacent owner","may apply to the Minister for permission"]}]; goal: Recall when an owner or agricultural land owners association may apply to construct a ditch or drain through an adjacent owner's property.
- group-2: Deposit condition and permission to construct ditch or drain; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:12","childLabels":["12(2)"],"evidenceText":["deposits with the Minister an amount of money","sufficient to cover all damages","the Minister may grant the applicant permission","shall pay out the sum so deposited"]}]; goal: Recall how a deposit supports permission to construct a ditch or drain and payment to entitled persons.
- group-3: Minister powers for ditch or drain construction; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:12","childLabels":["12(3)"],"evidenceText":["investigate the need for the ditch or drain","estimate the cost of the ditch or drain","attempt to seek an agreement among the interested parties","construct the ditch or drain whether or not the Minister obtains an agreement","assess each owner of the benefited land"]}]; goal: Recall the investigation, agreement, construction, and cost-assessment powers the Minister may use for ditch or drain construction.

- group-3: Employer inspection program and sharing of inspection results; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:9","childLabels":["9(3)"]}]; goal: Recall who must help develop the workplace inspection program and who receives inspection results.

## 802. Occupational Health and Safety Act s. 14
Disposition: split
Confidence: high
Priority: P1
Reason: Section 14 contains distinct committee topics: when a committee must be established and how it is composed, how meetings and records work, and how unresolved matters reach an officer.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Non-project-site committee establishment, composition, representation, and co-chairs; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:14","childLabels":["14(0.1)","14(1)","14(2)","14(3)","14(4)","14(5)"]}]; goal: Recall when a non-project-site joint health and safety committee is required and how its membership and co-chairs are determined.
- group-2: Committee meetings, minutes, posting, retention, and Commission access; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:14","childLabels":["14(6)","14(8)","14(9)","14(10)"]}]; goal: Identify the meeting, minute, posting, retention and Commission-access duties for a committee.

## 803. Occupational Health and Safety Act s. 16
Disposition: standalone
Confidence: high
Priority: P3
Reason: Section 16 permits the Commission to reduce meeting frequency for low-risk employment if safety is not materially affected, and to schedule meeting times where scheduled meetings would disrupt operations.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Commission changes to committee meeting frequency or timing; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:16","childLabels":["16(1)","16(2)"]}]; goal: Recall when the Commission may reduce committee meeting frequency or schedule meeting times.

## 804. Occupational Health and Safety Act s. 17
Disposition: standalone
Confidence: high
Priority: P2
Reason: Section 17 excludes project sites, requires a safety policy for employers with 5 to 19 employees that may include a representative, allows the Commission to require a representative policy for high-risk or high-accident workplaces, and requires election and posting where a representative is provided.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Health and safety representatives for non-project sites; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:17","childLabels":["17(0.1)","17(1)","17(2)","17(3)","17(4)"]}]; goal: Recall when non-project workplaces use health and safety representatives and how representatives are elected and posted.

## 805. Occupational Health and Safety Act s. 18
Disposition: standalone
Confidence: high
Priority: P2
Reason: Section 18 lets a health and safety representative do what a committee may do under section 15, requires regular consultation with the employer, and requires calling an officer if no agreement is reached on a health or safety matter.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Functions and dispute resolution for health and safety representatives; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:18","childLabels":["18(1)","18(2)","18(3)"]}]; goal: Recall the representative functions, employer consultation duty, and officer referral rule for unresolved health and safety matters.

## 806. Occupational Health and Safety Act s. 20
Disposition: split
Confidence: high
Priority: P1
Reason: Section 20 is a staged refusal-to-work process with supervisor investigation, committee referral, officer referral and remedial orders, plus employee availability while investigations or appeals are pending.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Supervisor investigation and remedial action after an employee safety report; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:20","childLabels":["20(1)","20(2)","20(3)"]}]; goal: Recall the supervisor-stage duties after an employee reports that an act may endanger health or safety.
- group-2: Committee investigation and recommendation after unresolved refusal; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:20","childLabels":["20(4)","20(5)","20(6)","20(7)"]}]; goal: Explain the committee-stage process when the employee safety concern is not resolved with the supervisor.
- group-3: Officer findings, orders, written advice, appeal link, and employee availability; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:20","childLabels":["20(8)","20(9)","20(10)","20(11)","20(11.1)","20(12)"]}]; goal: Identify the officer-stage findings, remedial order or advice, appeal connection, and employee availability requirements after a work refusal.

## 807. Occupational Health and Safety Act s. 21
Disposition: standalone
Confidence: high
Priority: P1
Reason: Section 21 states how long the right to refuse remains protected through supervisor, committee, officer and Chief Compliance Officer appeal stages, and prevents assigning another employee unless that employee is advised of the refusal, reasons and rights.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Protection period for refused work and limits on assigning another employee; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:21","childLabels":["21(1)","21(2)"]}]; goal: Recall when an employee refusal remains protected and what an employer or supervisor must tell another employee before assigning the refused act.

## 808. Occupational Health and Safety Act s. 22
Disposition: standalone
Confidence: high
Priority: P2
Reason: Section 22 permits temporary reassignment to reasonably equivalent work while a protected refusal continues, with same wages and benefits, subject to any collective agreement.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Temporary reassignment during protected work refusal; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:22","childLabels":["22(1)","22(2)"]}]; goal: Recall when and how an employee may be reassigned during a protected work refusal and what pay and agreement limits apply.

## 809. Occupational Health and Safety Act s. 24
Disposition: standalone
Confidence: high
Priority: P1
Reason: Section 24 prohibits employers, supervisors and unions from discriminatory action, threats, intimidation or coercion because an employee sought enforcement or complied with the Act, regulations, orders, or workplace-related Smoke-free Places Act requirements, and excludes section 22 reassignment from discriminatory action.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Prohibition on discriminatory action for enforcing or complying with safety laws; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:24","childLabels":["24(1)","24(2)"]}]; goal: Identify the prohibited reprisals for safety-law enforcement or compliance and the reassignment exclusion.

## 810. Occupational Health and Safety Act s. 25
Disposition: standalone
Confidence: high
Priority: P1
Reason: Section 25 lets an employee complaining of a section 24 violation choose final and binding collective-agreement arbitration, if available, or file a written complaint with the Commission within one year, after which the Commission refers timely complaints to an appointed arbitrator.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Complaint path and time limit for discriminatory action; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:25","childLabels":["25(1)","25(1.1)","25(2)"]}]; goal: Recall how an employee brings a discriminatory-action complaint, the filing deadline, and the Commission referral duty.

Disposition: split
Confidence: high
Priority: P1

Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Arbitrator powers, discriminatory-action findings, and remedial orders; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:26","childLabels":["26(1)","26(2)","26(3)"]}]; goal: Recall the arbitrator powers, available remedies, and written finding requirements for discriminatory-action complaints.
- group-2: Clarification requests and court review of arbitrator orders; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:26","childLabels":["26(4)","26(5)","26(6)","26(7)","26(9)","26(10)","26(11)"]}]; goal: Identify how arbitrator orders may be clarified or reviewed by the Court of King Bench and what procedural rules apply.
- group-3: Meaning of arbitrator in discriminatory-action proceedings; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:26","childLabels":["26(12)"]}]; goal: Recall who counts as the arbitrator for section 26.

## 812. Occupational Health and Safety Act s. 27
Disposition: standalone
Confidence: high
Priority: P2
Reason: Section 27 permits the employee or Commission to file an arbitrator order in the Court of King Bench after the review period expires if a bound party fails to comply, and makes the filed order enforceable as a court judgment.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Court filing and judgment enforcement of arbitrator orders; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:27","childLabels":["27(1)","27(2)"]}]; goal: Recall how an arbitrator order is filed and enforced as a judgment when a party does not comply.

Confidence: high
Priority: P1

Conflicts: none

- group-2: Technical expert assistance and evidentiary use of certified copies; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:28","childLabels":["28(2)","28(3)"]}]; goal: Recall how technical experts may assist officers and how certified copies are used as evidence.

## 814. Occupational Health and Safety Act s. 31
Disposition: standalone
Confidence: high
Priority: P2
Reason: Section 31 allows an officer to give oral or written orders to any person for matters regulated, controlled or required by the Act or regulations, set a completion time, and requires oral orders to be written before the officer leaves the place of employment.
Warnings: none
Conflicts: none
Proposed groups:
- group-1: Officer oral and written orders for Act or regulation matters; focus: [{"sourceKey":"section:31","childLabels":["31(1)","31(2)"]}]; goal: Recall when an officer may give orders, how timing may be specified, and when an oral order must be reduced to writing.

Confidence: high
